# Proposed OEIS comments: A189181 to A198405

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A189181

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189181>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2873](../papers/02501-03000/02873-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 50*a(n-1) +189*a(n-2) -17418*a(n-3) +111208*a(n-4) +452547*a(n-5) -4449430*a(n-6) +190937*a(n-7) +29532742*a(n-8) -4370254*a(n-9) +186074662*a(n-10) -567421032*a(n-11) -487743654*a(n-12) -150818946*a(n-13) -265001610*a(n-14) +6374532886*a(n-15) -3311165152*a(n-16) -3325788742*a(n-17) +2609479826*a(n-18) -1073359914*a(n-19) +294380347*a(n-20) +4801462*a(n-21) -20168455*a(n-22) +4712116*a(n-23) -541926*a(n-24) -30393*a(n-25) +13302*a(n-26) -675*a(n-27)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 11 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 126 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 26. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. That makes a(n) a walk count on 615 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A189605

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189605>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3359](../papers/03001-03500/03359-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) -3*a(n-2) -12*a(n-3) -11*a(n-4) +34*a(n-5) -27*a(n-6) +9*a(n-7) +7*a(n-8) +49*a(n-9) -82*a(n-10) +84*a(n-11) -54*a(n-12) +21*a(n-13) -6*a(n-14) +a(n-15)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 10 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 120 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 14. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A193641

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A193641>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [5302](../papers/05001-05500/05302-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Or yet empirical: row sums of triangle
> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-3).
> Empirical: G.f.: -x*(1+x+x^2) / ( -1+2*x+x^3 ); a(n) = A008998(n-3) + A008998(n-2) + A008998(n-1). - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 19 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A193703>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [4187](../papers/04001-04500/04187-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +a(n-2) +2*a(n-3) -a(n-4) +3*a(n-6) +4*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + x)^2*(1 - x + 3*x^2 - x^3 + 4*x^4) / (1 - 2*x - x^2 - 2*x^3 + x^4 - 3*x^6 - 4*x^7). - _Colin Barker_, May 04 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 49 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A194932

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A194932>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5443](../papers/05001-05500/05443-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 171*n - 127 for n>2.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(64 + 89*x + 16*x^2 + 2*x^3) / (1 - x)^2. - _Colin Barker_, May 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and it is exact rather than eventual guesswork. The shape is fixed and it is the alphabet 0..n that grows, so this is not a walk count. The condition only ever compares two entries, so adding a constant to every entry keeps an array admissible: the arrays fall into translation classes, and a class whose largest and smallest entries differ by s contributes max(0, n+1-s) arrays with entries in 0..n. There are finitely many classes, because any two cells are joined by a path of at most D edges and each edge moves the value by at most d, so no class has s > d*D. For n at least that bound every term is positive and a(n) is exactly linear, so computing a(n) at two points fixes it for good. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A197230

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197230>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1353](../papers/01001-01500/01353-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +3*a(n-2) +38*a(n-3) +40*a(n-4) +10*a(n-5) -150*a(n-6) -375*a(n-7) -175*a(n-8) +637*a(n-9) +2013*a(n-10) +1368*a(n-11) -1614*a(n-12) -4361*a(n-13) -4043*a(n-14) +946*a(n-15) +4156*a(n-16) +3551*a(n-17) +552*a(n-18) -881*a(n-19) -629*a(n-20) -1033*a(n-21) +508*a(n-22).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This empirical recurrence is false. The entry publishes 22 terms with offset 1, so the recurrence first says something at n = 23, and it already fails there: the true value is 1327965802062332 and the recurrence gives 1327965802062198. The sequence is a transfer-matrix count, hence C-finite, and its true minimal recurrence has order 25; its first 22 coefficients are exactly the ones above, so the line is the correct recurrence with its last three terms dropped. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
